--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 1:**
- The champion is a 1-state GoAwayColor (clr=6=cyan) machine with score -1.79. Cyan corresponds to one wall color. The arena has BLUE (clr=3) and GREEN (clr=2) walls behind the source halves, and RED (clr=1) on the nest side. Wait - let me reconsider the color mapping: {1,2,3,4,5,6} likely maps to {red, green, blue, yellow, magenta, cyan}.
- GoAwayColor cyan (6) scores -1.79: robots flee cyan walls, which pushes them toward the opposite side. If cyan is one source wall color, fleeing it might accidentally drive robots to the nest consistently.
- Actually: RED wall is on nest side (clr=1). BLUE (clr=3) and GREEN (clr=2) are behind source halves. The remaining colors for walls might be cyan (6) for some boundary.

**Strategy:**
- The best behavior is GoAwayColor clr=6 (cyan). We need to understand: fleeing cyan means robots go toward... the nest (white floor) side if cyan marks the source walls, or toward the source if cyan is the nest wall.
- Score is -1.79 meaning robots are consistently delivering the GOOD item type. 
- We should exploit this: add states that navigate back after collecting items, and try GoAwayColor with other colors.

**Key insight:** GoAwayColor clr=6 works. Let's try:
1. Survival: exact copy of champion
2. Exploit: Try GoAwayColor with other colors (1,2,3,4,5)
3. Multi-state: GoAwayColor → detect floor color → switch behavior
4. Try GoToColor clr=1 (red=nest wall) to go to nest, combined with GoAwayColor

**Multi-state ideas:**
- State 0: GoAwayColor cyan (flee source area) → when on WHITE floor (nest), switch to GoToColor blue/green (go collect)
- State 0: GoToColor red (go to nest) → when on BLACK floor, switch to GoToColor red
- State 0: GoAwayColor cyan → on WHITE, switch to GoToColor green/blue → on BLACK, switch back

Let me design 14 machines:

1. Champion (survival)
2. GoAwayColor clr=5 (magenta)
3. GoAwayColor clr=3 (blue) - flee blue source wall
4. GoAwayColor clr=2 (green) - flee green source wall  
5. GoAwayColor clr=1 (red) - flee red/nest wall → goes to source
6. GoToColor clr=1 (go toward red/nest wall)
7. 2-state: GoAwayColor cyan + GoToColor green (on white floor go collect green)
8. 2-state: GoAwayColor cyan + GoToColor blue
9. 2-state: GoToColor red (to nest) ↔ GoToColor green (to source)
10. 2-state: GoToColor red ↔ GoToColor blue
11. 3-state foraging: GoToColor green (collect) → on white floor GoToColor red (deliver) → on black GoToColor green
12. 3-state: GoAwayColor cyan → on white GoToColor green → on black GoAwayColor cyan
13. Exploration with floor-color switching
14. 2-state GoAwayColor + attraction

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 2:**
- Champion: GoAwayColor clr=2 (GREEN) scores -8.63. This means fleeing the GREEN wall drives robots consistently to deliver good items (from the GREEN half, items decrease score by 1). Wait - fleeing GREEN means robots move AWAY from green walls (the green source half), ending up at the nest side. So they pick up items from the GREEN half (score -1 each) and deliver them to nest. That's the good behavior!
- Actually: robots flee green → they spend time near the nest (white floor) → but they need to pick up items first. Maybe the behavior is: flee green → go to blue side → pick up blue items → wander back? Or: flee green → oscillate between source and nest?
- GoAwayColor clr=3 (BLUE) scored +7.80 - very bad! So fleeing blue drives robots to deliver the WRONG item type (green items increase score).
- The 2-state machine (GoAwayColor cyan → on white switch to GoToColor blue) scored -6.88, second best.

**What's happening:** GoAwayColor GREEN (clr=2) = flee green walls = robots go toward nest side OR toward blue side. They pick up blue items (score -1) and deliver them. This is the optimal behavior!

**Strategy for Round 3:**
1. Survival: exact copy of champion (GoAwayColor green)
2. Exploit champion: add transitions to enhance delivery cycle
3. Try: GoAwayColor green + when on WHITE floor (nest, delivered) → GoToColor blue (go collect blue)
4. Try: GoAwayColor green + when on BLACK floor (source) → GoAwayColor green (continue)
5. Multi-state with floor detection to make explicit foraging cycle
6. Try GoToColor blue (clr=3) directly
7. Avoid: GoToColor green, GoAwayColor blue

**Multi-state foraging cycle:**
- State 0 (collect): GoToColor blue (clr=3) → when on BLACK floor → go to deliver
- State 1 (deliver): GoToColor red (clr=1, nest wall) → when on WHITE floor → go collect
- OR: GoAwayColor green → on WHITE → GoToColor blue → on BLACK → GoAwayColor green

FSM: --nstates 1 --s0 9 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 0 --clr0 2

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 3:**
- Champion: 2-state machine: GoToColor BLUE (clr=3) → on BLACK floor switch to GoAwayColor GREEN (clr=2) → on WHITE floor switch back. Score -12.73!
- The behavior: GoToColor blue (go to blue source half) → pick up blue item (black floor) → GoAwayColor green (flee green wall = move toward nest) → on white floor (nest) deliver → switch back to GoToColor blue.
- This is a clean foraging cycle: collect blue items, deliver to nest!
- Note: GoToColor blue alone (clr=3) scored +7.66 - terrible! So the key is the combination.
- The 3-state version with GoToColor red (nest wall) in between scored -11.62.

**Champion topology analysis:**
- S0: GoToColor blue (clr=3), transitions: on BLACK (p=0.9) → S0 (self? no wait - destination 0 means same state index? No - with 2 states, from state 0, destination 0 = state 1), on WHITE (p=0.3) → S0
- Wait: with 2 states, from state 0, `--n0x0 0` means destination index 0 among OTHER states = state 1. So:
  - S0 GoToColor blue: on BLACK(p=0.9)→S1, on WHITE(p=0.3)→S1
  - S1 GoAwayColor green: on WHITE(p=0.9)→S0, on BLACK(p=0.2)→S0

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Exact champion copy (survival)
2. Tweak probabilities: p=0.95/0.25, p=0.85/0.35, etc.
3. Add third state (exploration/randomness buffer)
4. Try different transition triggers (gray floor, neighbor counts)
5. Try GoToColor blue + GoToColor red (nest) instead of GoAwayColor green
6. Try swapping: GoAwayColor green first, then GoToColor blue

**Exploration:**
- 3-state: GoToColor blue → (black floor) → GoToColor red → (white floor) → GoAwayColor green → (black) → GoToColor blue
- 3-state: GoToColor blue → (black) → GoAwayColor green → (white) → GoToColor blue with gray floor trigger
- Add neighbor-based transitions

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 4:**
- The champion (-12.73) and top performers are all variations of the same 2-state topology: GoToColor BLUE (clr=3) ↔ GoAwayColor GREEN (clr=2), with floor-color transitions (BLACK and WHITE).
- The parameter space seems well-explored around p=0.85-0.95 for the main transitions and p=0.15-0.35 for the secondary ones.
- 4-state machines with extra states are performing poorly. 
- The 3-state version with GoToColor RED (nest wall) in between scored -11.15 - slightly worse than 2-state.
- Color-camera transitions (C=7) hurt performance.

**The core pattern:**
- S0: GoToColor blue → [BLACK→S1, WHITE→S1]  (go collect, transition when on source or nest)
- S1: GoAwayColor green → [WHITE→S0, BLACK→S0]  (flee green=go to nest, deliver)

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Fine-tune probabilities around the champion: try p=0.92/0.28, p=0.88/0.32, p=0.93/0.22, etc.
- Try asymmetric probabilities: high p for BLACK in S0 (definitely switch when collected), lower for WHITE; high p for WHITE in S1 (definitely switch when delivered)
- Try adding a third transition (gray floor) to help with navigation between areas
- Try p=1.0 for the primary triggers (always switch on the right floor color)

**Exploration:**
- Try with only 1 transition per state (simplify) but with p very high
- Try different combinations: GoToColor blue + GoToColor red (to nest)
- Try GoAwayColor green + GoToColor blue with swapped state ordering

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 6:**
- New champion: -12.97. The key change was reducing the secondary transition probability from 0.3 to 0.05 (WHITE floor in S0, BLACK floor in S1). This makes sense: the secondary transitions are "escape" transitions for when robots are on the wrong floor, but too high a probability causes premature switching.
- The champion: S0 GoToColor blue → BLACK(p=0.9)→S1, WHITE(p=0.05)→S1; S1 GoAwayColor green → WHITE(p=0.9)→S0, BLACK(p=0.05)→S0
- The pattern is clear: high probability on the "correct" floor trigger, very low on the "wrong" floor trigger
- Lower secondary probabilities (0.05, 0.10) work better than higher ones (0.25, 0.30)

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Try secondary probability = 0.0 (remove it entirely, use 1 transition)
2. Try secondary probability = 0.02, 0.03, 0.07, 0.08
3. Try asymmetric: high primary for both but different secondary values
4. Try p=0.92/0.05, p=0.95/0.05, p=0.85/0.05
5. The 3-state version with GoToColor red scored -12.55 - try with lower secondary probs

**Exploration:**
- Try completely removing secondary transitions (already tried 1-transition, got -11.88)
- Try different primary probabilities with very low secondary
- Try the 3-state topology with the new insight (lower secondary p)

The key insight is: low secondary probability (~0.05) is better than high (~0.3). Let me fine-tune around p_secondary ∈ {0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.07, 0.10} while keeping primary at 0.9.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 7:**
- Champion remains at -12.97. The parameter space around this topology is well-explored.
- Top performers all use the same 2-state topology with p_primary ~0.9-0.95 and p_secondary ~0.03-0.07.
- The 3-state version with GoToColor red scored -12.77 - competitive but not beating champion.
- Adding extra transitions hurts (gray floor transition in S1 dropped to -4.19).
- 4-state machines with exploration states continue to perform poorly.

**Parameter analysis:**
- Primary p: 0.90 (champion), 0.95 (second best) both work well
- Secondary p: 0.05 (champion), 0.07, 0.03 all similar
- The champion has p_primary=0.9 for both states and p_secondary=0.05 for both
- Best performer #2 has asymmetric: S0 p_primary=0.9, S1 p_primary=0.95 → -12.81

**We seem near a local optimum.** Let me try:
1. Survival: exact champion copy
2. Fine-grained exploration: try p_primary in {0.88, 0.90, 0.92, 0.93, 0.95, 0.97} × p_secondary in {0.01, 0.02, 0.04, 0.05, 0.06}
3. Asymmetric: different primaries for S0 and S1
4. Explore: try completely different topologies to escape local optima
   - GoToColor blue + GoToColor red (both going toward walls)
   - Try with p_secondary = 0.0 (not possible, but try 0.01)
   - Try GoAwayColor green alone with floor transitions

**Genuine exploration:**
- Try: S0 GoToColor blue (go collect) → on BLACK go to S1; S1 GoToColor red (go to nest) → on WHITE go to S0
- Try: different color combinations
- Try neighbor-count transitions instead of floor color

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Major breakthrough in Round 8!**
- New champion: -14.15! The key change: S1 is now **GoToColor RED (clr=1)** instead of GoAwayColor green!
- Champion topology: S0=GoToColor blue (clr=3) → BLACK(p=0.9)→S1, WHITE(p=0.06)→S1; S1=GoToColor red (clr=1) → WHITE(p=0.9)→S0, BLACK(p=0.06)→S0
- Interpretation: Go to blue source wall (collect blue item) → on black floor switch to GoToColor red (nest wall) → move toward red nest wall → on white floor (delivered!) switch back
- This is a much cleaner foraging cycle: explicitly navigate TO the blue source, then explicitly navigate TO the red nest wall!

**Why this works better than GoAwayColor green:**
- GoToColor red actively steers toward the nest wall (red), which is more direct than fleeing green
- Both behaviors are "go toward" which gives cleaner directional guidance

**Exploitation strategy:**
1. Survival: exact champion copy
2. Fine-tune secondary probability: try 0.04, 0.05, 0.07, 0.08
3. Fine-tune primary probability: try 0.88, 0.92, 0.95
4. Asymmetric: different p values for S0 and S1
5. Try GoToColor blue + GoToColor red with only 1 transition each (simpler)
6. Try 3-state version with this new topology

**Exploration:**
- Try swapping: S0=GoToColor red → S1=GoToColor blue
- Try adding a third state (exploration buffer between source and nest)
- Try different secondary p values: 0.01, 0.02, 0.03

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 9:**
- Champion improved to -14.40! Same topology but now confirmed: S0=GoToColor blue (clr=3), S1=GoToColor red (clr=1), both with p_primary=0.9 and p_secondary=0.06.
- Two machines tied at -14.40: p=0.9/0.06 and p=0.88/0.06. The secondary probability 0.06 is the sweet spot.
- The 1-transition version scored only -12.28 (worse), confirming the secondary transition matters.
- 3-state versions consistently underperform the 2-state version.
- Adding extra states or exploration hurts.

**This is the final round.** We need to maximize exploitation while keeping some exploration.

**Fine-tuning around the champion:**
- p_primary: try 0.86, 0.87, 0.88, 0.89, 0.90, 0.91, 0.92
- p_secondary: try 0.04, 0.05, 0.06, 0.07, 0.08
- Asymmetric: S0 p_secondary vs S1 p_secondary
- Try p_primary=0.88 (tied with champion) with different secondaries

**The pattern:** p_secondary=0.06 seems optimal. Let me explore p_primary more carefully around 0.88-0.92, and also try slight asymmetries between S0 and S1.

**Survival:** Exact champion copy mandatory.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 16 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.76 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
